(dynamic workflow)","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"design","description":"Grant or revoke Claude agent access to your Design projects","argumentHint":"consent | revoke","builtin":true},{"name":"design-sync","description":"Push a React design system to claude.ai/design. This runs a converter that bundles the real component code (from Storybook or a bare package) and uploads it. Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace.","argumentHint":"[interval] [prompt]","aliases":["proactive"],"builtin":true},{"name":"schedule","description":"Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["routines"],"builtin":true},{"name":"claude-api","description":"Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"workflow-authoring","description":"Reference for writing a Workflow tool script (script API and gotchas, resume, quality patterns, worked examples). Claude desktop app (also called the in-app browser, the browser pane, Claude's browser, or \"your own browser\"), whose tools are named mcp__Claude_Browser__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (editable docs people share and comment on; the default for any document, named as a doc or not: a document, report, proposal, resume, cover letter, letter, contract, policy, form, template, worksheet, essay, handbook, guide, how-to, cheat sheet, SOP or other writing to keep, share, collaborate on, send, submit, print or sign; a doc exports to Word, PDF, Markdown or Google Docs, so needing a file to send, attach, upload, submit or print is no reason to pick Word, and a file nobody asked for is a doc, not Word; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat; a pasted claude.ai artifact link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; Word or another file format named, tracked changes wanted, or a .docx to change or use as a template → that format's skill): making one → if no docs-connector instructions are in context, call its `guide` (topic.instructions) first; then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of Microsoft Word Documents, such as 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', 'microsoft doc'. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead, even if they will email or print it. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:google-workspace","description":"Read this before the first Google Drive, Docs, Sheets or Slides connector call whenever the task creates or changes a Google file. Use this skill whenever the user wants to create or change a Google Doc, Sheet or Slides file in their Google Drive. Triggers include: a request that names Google Docs, Sheets, Slides or Drive and asks to make, edit, format, copy or rename a file; a docs.google.com link with a request to change that file, even a one-line fix or suggested edits; and any follow-up change to a Google file from earlier in the chat, even \"change it\" or \"add a tab\". Includes helper scripts for document positions, cell ranges and slide layout. However, if the user asks for a doc, deck or spreadsheet without naming Google, or gives a Google file only as source material for something new, use Claude's own output type instead. Do NOT use for read-only questions about a Google file, or for Word, Excel, PowerPoint or PDF files. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["google-workspace"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"3417fde1-0660-4cd3-8354-68150fa27328","session_id":"8106c64f-84fe-4fc2-85a1-3e610198f667"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CfkmAPXVrkmiVjwnZdAWM","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01UumP8F3KDdN2ghY85eAZai","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-primary-planner-1ca79b2cdd2f/.github/workflows/aria-auto-cycle.yml","offset":207,"limit":400},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":111853,"cache_read_input_tokens":0,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":111853},"output_tokens":5,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8106c64f-84fe-4fc2-85a1-3e610198f667","uuid":"3b44aa41-4f32-4d7f-a2b6-cbb64a5b7f50","timestamp":"2026-10-06T09:42:24.601Z","request_id":"req_011CfkmAP5huHCGG3A3ppUZS","wire_tool_inputs":{"toolu_01UumP8F3KDdN2ghY85eAZai":{"file_path":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-primary-planner-1ca79b2cdd2f/.github/workflows/aria-auto-cycle.yml","offset":207,"limit":400}}}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UumP8F3KDdN2ghY85eAZai","type":"tool_result","content":"207\t      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n208\t        with:\n209\t          ref: main\n210\t          persist-credentials: false\n211\t          # The kernel reads this checkout's HISTORY, not only its tree: the\n212\t          # twin's churn and co-change layers replay `git log -n400`, the\n213\t          # incremental refresh asks whether the prior map's anchor commit\n214\t          # exists, the executor's anchor gate asks the same of a previous\n215\t          # run's commit, and intent context reads per-file logs.\n216\t          # actions/checkout defaults to depth 1, and on this persistent\n217\t          # workspace a depth-1 fetch RE-SHALLOWS a full clone, so the run\n218\t          # that wrote the live map (2026-09-12) counted one commit: churn\n219\t          # and co-change never reached their recurrence thresholds and an\n220\t          # empty history layer was published as a healthy map, while every\n221\t          # refresh was a full rebuild (`refresh.reason=unknown_anchor`).\n222\t          # 0 = the whole history; on an already-shallow workspace the\n223\t          # action fetches `--unshallow`. The kernel refuses a shallow\n224\t          # checkout by name (`twin_history_unavailable_shallow`) and\n225\t          # aria-kernel/tests/invariants/test_kernel_lanes_check_out_full_history.py\n226\t          # pins this flag on every lane that runs the kernel. The\n227\t          # aria/state store is NOT this checkout: the restore action\n228\t          # materialises it through the kernel's own fetch (no depth) into a\n229\t          # sibling worktree, and its history length is governed by\n230\t          # aria-state-maintenance, not by this step.\n231\t          fetch-depth: 0\n232\t          # Self-hosted workspace persists between ALL jobs (CI, executor,\n233\t          # this lane). A dispatch-triggered run inherits whatever the\n234\t          # previous job left behind; the mid-run preflight's\n235\t          # workspace_worktree_not_clean guard then rejects the run for\n236\t          # files this lane never created. The reset step above already put\n237\t          # the tree back to the committed state, so checkout does not repeat\n238\t          # it with the one flag that also throws the dependencies away.\n239\t          clean: false\n240\t\n241\t      # ARIA-HIGH-019 — anchor the job's wall-clock at the earliest possible\n242\t      # step, BEFORE checkout and provisioning: every setup minute spent\n243\t      # before the epoch is a minute stolen from the close-out margin. The\n244\t      # nightly step used to compute its seal deadline from its OWN start\n245\t      # time, assuming ~5m of setup before it; a fresh-store restore of the\n246\t      # aria/state branch measures 17m41s (run 32771477211), which spent\n247\t      # the whole close-out margin before the first cycle phase and pushed the\n248\t      # seal 8 minutes past the 360m platform ceiling — the job was cancelled\n249\t      # between seal and publish (again on run 33172463619, cancelled at the\n250\t      # ceiling mid-handoff with the anchor still after setup).\n251\t      - name: Anchor the job launch epoch\n252\t        run: echo \"ARIA_JOB_LAUNCH_EPOCH=$(date +%s)\" >> \"$GITHUB_ENV\"\n253\t\n254\t      - uses: ./.github/actions/setup-aria-kernel\n255\t\n256\t      - uses: actions/setup-node@820762786026740c76f36085b0efc47a31fe5020 # v7.0.0\n257\t        with:\n258\t          node-version: '22'\n259\t\n260\t      # Same dependency step as aria-operational-proof — the fresh\n261\t      # tool-cache Python on the self-hosted runner ships without the\n262\t      # kernel's deps (first live run failed importing yaml inside\n263\t      # verify_workflow_preflight, ORPHAN-LOW-302).\n264\t      - name: Pre-flight - Claude Code CLI present\n265\t        run: |\n266\t          set -euo pipefail\n267\t          REQUIRED_CLAUDE_VERSION=\"2.1.221\"\n268\t          CLAUDE_VERSION=\"$(claude --version | awk '{print $1}')\"\n269\t          python3 - \"$REQUIRED_CLAUDE_VERSION\" \"$CLAUDE_VERSION\" <<'PY'\n270\t          import sys\n271\t          def parse(value):\n272\t              return tuple(int(part) for part in value.split('.'))\n273\t          required = parse(sys.argv[1])\n274\t          current = parse(sys.argv[2])\n275\t          if current < required:\n276\t              raise SystemExit(f\"claude-code {sys.argv[2]} is below required {sys.argv[1]}\")\n277\t          print(f\"claude-code {sys.argv[2]} satisfies required {sys.argv[1]}\")\n278\t          PY\n279\t\n280\t      - name: Pre-flight - Claude Code auth + mock-mode kill-switch resolution\n281\t        id: preflight\n282\t        env:\n283\t          KILL_SWITCH: ${{ vars.ARIA_MOCK_KILL_SWITCH }}\n284\t          INPUT_MOCK: ${{ github.event.inputs.mock }}\n285\t        run: |\n286\t          set -euo pipefail\n287\t          if [ -n \"${KILL_SWITCH:-}\" ]; then\n288\t            EFFECTIVE_MOCK=\"${KILL_SWITCH}\"\n289\t            MOCK_SOURCE=\"kill_switch\"\n290\t          elif [ -n \"${INPUT_MOCK:-}\" ]; then\n291\t            EFFECTIVE_MOCK=\"${INPUT_MOCK}\"\n292\t            MOCK_SOURCE=\"workflow_dispatch_input\"\n293\t          else\n294\t            EFFECTIVE_MOCK=\"false\"\n295\t            MOCK_SOURCE=\"workflow_default_claude\"\n296\t          fi\n297\t          # Plan 032 Faz 032a — the kill switch is a raw repository variable\n298\t          # and the kernel's boolean parser raises on anything but\n299\t          # true/false, so a value like `enabled` would not force mock: it\n300\t          # would crash the executor after the claim. Refuse it HERE, where\n301\t          # the operator can read the reason, and name the source.\n302\t          case \"${EFFECTIVE_MOCK}\" in\n303\t            true|false) ;;\n304\t            *)\n305\t              echo \"::error::ARIA mock resolution from ${MOCK_SOURCE} is '${EFFECTIVE_MOCK}'; only 'true' or 'false' is accepted\" >&2\n306\t              exit 1\n307\t              ;;\n308\t          esac\n309\t          echo \"effective_mock=${EFFECTIVE_MOCK}\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n310\t          echo \"mock_source=${MOCK_SOURCE}\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n311\t          if [ \"${EFFECTIVE_MOCK}\" = \"false\" ]; then\n312\t            if env | grep -E '^(ANTHROPIC_API_KEY|CLAUDE_API_KEY|ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN)=' >/dev/null; then\n313\t              echo \"::error::API-key Claude mode is disallowed for ARIA; use a managed Claude Code login on the trusted runner.\" >&2\n314\t              exit 1\n315\t            fi\n316\t            claude --version >/tmp/aria-claude-auth.txt 2>/tmp/aria-claude-auth.err \\\n317\t              || { echo \"::error::Claude Code CLI preflight failed\" >&2; cat /tmp/aria-claude-auth.err >&2; exit 1; }\n318\t            CLAUDE_AUTH_DIR=\"${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}\"\n319\t            if [ ! -f \"${CLAUDE_AUTH_DIR}/.credentials.json\" ] && [ ! -f \"${CLAUDE_AUTH_DIR}/config.json\" ]; then\n320\t              echo \"::error::Claude Code managed-auth session not found on the trusted runner\" >&2\n321\t              exit 1\n322\t            fi\n323\t          fi\n324\t\n325\t      - name: Persist enterprise workflow preflight\n326\t        env:\n327\t          PYTHONPATH: aria-kernel\n328\t        run: |\n329\t          set -euo pipefail\n330\t          python3 - <<'PY'\n331\t          import os\n332\t          from pathlib import Path\n333\t\n334\t          from aria_kernel.preflight import verify_workflow_preflight\n335\t          from aria_kernel.runtime_profile import SCHEDULER_MAX_PROPOSABLE_PROFILE\n336\t\n337\t          # PLAN Wave 1 PR 2.6b — ONE declared write root, because after the\n338\t          # cutover there is one. The cycle's three state roots (tools,\n339\t          # workspace, findings) all live inside the store worktree, so the\n340\t          # pair of checkout-relative roots this used to declare would now\n341\t          # be write roots nothing writes to — a declaration that stopped\n342\t          # describing the run. Everything outside the store stays read-only,\n343\t          # which is a STRONGER claim than before: `aria-findings/` in the\n344\t          # checkout is no longer written at all.\n345\t          allowed = [\".aria-state-store\"]\n346\t          verdict = verify_workflow_preflight(\n347\t              workflow_id=\"aria-auto-cycle\",\n348\t              job_id=\"cycle\",\n349\t              # ORPHAN-HIGH-728 — this preflight runs BEFORE the state store\n350\t              # is bound, so it cannot know tonight's profile; what it can\n351\t              # declare honestly is the lane's STRUCTURAL ceiling, the most\n352\t              # any resolution here could ever produce. Read from the kernel\n353\t              # rather than written as a literal, so a constant that moves\n354\t              # moves this declaration with it. The profile the run actually\n355\t              # took is declared by the second preflight after the resolver,\n356\t              # and recorded by `set_profile` in runtime-profile-history.jsonl.\n357\t              profile=SCHEDULER_MAX_PROPOSABLE_PROFILE,\n358\t              workspace_root=os.environ[\"GITHUB_WORKSPACE\"],\n359\t              allowed_write_roots=allowed,\n360\t              path_allowlist=allowed,\n361\t              # `github_git` is what the cutover added to this lane's reach:\n362\t              # the state branch is fetched and pushed over the repository\n363\t              # remote. Declared rather than folded into `github_artifact`,\n364\t              # because they are different credentials with different blast\n365\t              # radii and a reviewer must be able to see the git one.\n366\t              network_policy=[\"github_artifact\", \"github_git\"],\n367\t              network_enforcement_evidence=(\n368\t                  \"state restore/publish limited to the aria/state branch over \"\n369\t                  \"the repository remote; artifact upload limited to the \"\n370\t                  \"run-scoped aria-state-cache-<run_id> and \"\n371\t                  \"quarantine-evidence-<run_id> names, both via the Actions \"\n372\t                  \"artifact API\"\n373\t              ),\n374\t              token_provenance=\"github_actions_artifact_token\",\n375\t              require_github_app=False,\n376\t              dlp_mode=\"fail_closed\",\n377\t              dlp_scan_clean=True,\n378\t              audit_reason=(\n379\t                  \"nightly producer cycle over the durable aria/state store; \"\n380\t                  \"profile resolved per run from the L1 autonomy ladder \"\n381\t                  \"(strict when valid, standard otherwise)\"\n382\t              ),\n383\t              audit_artifact_path=Path(os.environ[\"RUNNER_TEMP\"]) / \"aria-auto-cycle-preflight.json\",\n384\t              external_root_allowlist=[str(Path(os.environ[\"RUNNER_TEMP\"]).resolve())],\n385\t          )\n386\t          if not verdict.valid:\n387\t              raise SystemExit(\";\".join(verdict.reasons))\n388\t          PY\n389\t\n390\t      - name: Restore ARIA state from the aria/state branch\n391\t        id: restore_state\n392\t        # RC-6 — one definition, both lanes. The two hand-copied heredocs that\n393\t        # used to sit here had already drifted: only the executor's carried the\n394\t        # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-484/488 restore-proof outputs, so the producer lane\n395\t        # could publish a bootstrap-empty tree over the accumulated state. The\n396\t        # action's header records what that cost.\n397\t        #\n398\t        # PLAN Wave 1 PR 2.6b — the transport is the durable store now, not the\n399\t        # 30-day artifact. The output contract is unchanged, deliberately: both\n400\t        # publish gates below still read `restored` / `bootstrap`. The action\n401\t        # also EXPORTS the store binding into the job environment, which is why\n402\t        # every `--tools-dir` below names \"$ARIA_TOOLS_DIR\" and never a literal.\n403\t        uses: ./.github/actions/restore-aria-state\n404\t        with:\n405\t          bootstrap-ack: ${{ vars.ARIA_STATE_BOOTSTRAP_ACK }}\n406\t          # ARIA-HIGH-342 — this job writes aria/state, so it takes the writer\n407\t          # lease before the checkout and holds it to the release step at the\n408\t          # end: no other lane can publish in between. The expiry is this\n409\t          # job's own timeout-minutes plus the 80-minute leased-publish margin\n410\t          # (GSEC-MEDIUM-003); the wait covers the hosted writers' whole\n411\t          # bounds (at most 25 min) before the run yields by name.\n412\t          writer-lease-ttl-minutes: '440'\n413\t          writer-lease-wait-seconds: '1800'\n414\t      - name: Pre-flight - cross-host autonomous-loop lease check\n415\t        id: lease_check\n416\t        env:\n417\t          WRITER_LEASE: ${{ steps.restore_state.outputs.writer-lease }}\n418\t        run: |\n419\t          set -euo pipefail\n420\t          PYTHONPATH=aria-kernel python3 - <<'PY' 2>&1 | head -20\n421\t          import json\n422\t          import os\n423\t          import sys\n424\t\n425\t          from aria_kernel.autonomous_host_lease import lease_state\n426\t\n427\t          # ARIA-HIGH-342 — no writer lease, no store: the restore yielded\n428\t          # (another lane is writing aria/state) and checked nothing out, so\n429\t          # this run blocks here, before anything reads the tree.\n430\t          if os.environ.get('WRITER_LEASE') != 'held':\n431\t              print('::warning::aria/state writer lease not held; run yielded', file=sys.stderr)\n432\t              with open(os.environ['GITHUB_OUTPUT'], 'a', encoding='utf-8') as f:\n433\t                  f.write('blocked=true\\n')\n434\t                  f.write('blocked_host=aria/state-writer-lease\\n')\n435\t              sys.exit(0)\n436\t\n437\t          # os.environ[...] rather than a literal: the lease ledger lives in\n438\t          # the store, and a KeyError here is the correct outcome if the\n439\t          # restore did not bind it. The literal would have read a DIFFERENT,\n440\t          # empty lease ledger in the ephemeral checkout and reported \"no\n441\t          # lease held\" — the exact false clear ORPHAN-CRITICAL-469 was.\n442\t          state = lease_state(os.environ['ARIA_TOOLS_DIR'])\n443\t          print(json.dumps(state))\n444\t          if state['state'] == 'fresh':\n445\t              host = state.get('host_id', '')\n446\t              if not host.startswith('gha-runner:'):\n447\t                  print(f'::warning::autonomous_host_lease_blocked: held by {host}', file=sys.stderr)\n448\t                  with open(os.environ['GITHUB_OUTPUT'], 'a', encoding='utf-8') as f:\n449\t                      f.write('blocked=true\\n')\n450\t                      f.write(f'blocked_host={host}\\n')\n451\t                  sys.exit(0)\n452\t          with open(os.environ['GITHUB_OUTPUT'], 'a', encoding='utf-8') as f:\n453\t              f.write('blocked=false\\n')\n454\t          PY\n455\t\n456\t      - name: Skip when local lease is fresh\n457\t        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked == 'true'\n458\t        run: |\n459\t          echo \"Local host holds autonomous-loop lease; exiting cleanly per Plan ARIA-V3 §2n.\"\n460\t          exit 0\n461\t\n462\t      # Every `--tools-dir` below names \"$ARIA_TOOLS_DIR\", the binding the\n463\t      # restore step exported, and never the literal `aria-tools`. The literal\n464\t      # is not merely stale — `aria-tools/repo_identity.json` is TRACKED in the\n465\t      # checkout, so it names a real, valid, and completely wrong tools root:\n466\t      # the run would succeed while writing every ledger to a tree that dies\n467\t      # with the runner. `set -u` turns an absent binding into a failed step,\n468\t      # which is the only acceptable alternative to the right root.\n469\t      #\n470\t      # The `integrity migrate-tools-bootstrap` step that used to sit here moved\n471\t      # INTO the restore action (PLAN Wave 1 PR 2.6b). It was this lane's alone,\n472\t      # and the executor needed it just as much: a store checkout is not a\n473\t      # usable tools root until it is bound. One definition, both lanes — the\n474\t      # same correction RC-6 made to the restore itself.\n475\t      - name: Handoff snapshot - session_start\n476\t        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true'\n477\t        run: |\n478\t          set -euo pipefail\n479\t          PYTHONPATH=aria-kernel python3 -m aria_kernel handoff snapshot \\\n480\t            --session-id \"auto-cycle-${GITHUB_RUN_ID}\" \\\n481\t            --tools-dir \"$ARIA_TOOLS_DIR\" \\\n482\t            --trigger session_start\n483\t\n484\t      - name: Fetch the composed supergraph for the drift scan\n485\t        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true' && (github.event.inputs.mode || 'cycle') == 'cycle'\n486\t        env:\n487\t          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}\n488\t        run: |\n489\t          set -euo pipefail\n490\t          # ARIA-HIGH-333 — the scan judges a UI option list against what the GraphQL transport carries, which it\n491\t          # reads from the composed supergraph. This host does not compose one; the artifact CI composed for HEAD's\n492\t          # exact schema is fetched instead. Without it the scan reports wire_unverifiable rather than guessing.\n493\t          SG_DIR=\"${RUNNER_TEMP}/aria-supergraph\"\n494\t          if out=\"$(python3 tools/aria-poc/fetch_supergraph.py --repo-root \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE\" --repo \"$GITHUB_REPOSITORY\" --out-dir \"$SG_DIR\")\"; then\n495\t            echo \"ARIA_SUPERGRAPH=${SG_DIR}/supergraph.graphql\" >> \"$GITHUB_ENV\"\n496\t          fi\n497\t          echo \"$out\"\n498\t\n499\t      - name: Seed drift findings from fresh scan\n500\t        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true' && (github.event.inputs.mode || 'cycle') == 'cycle'\n501\t        run: |\n502\t          set -euo pipefail\n503\t          # A fresh PoC mechanical scan at HEAD feeds the empty-cycle guard and\n504\t          # the pressure pipeline with real, never-stale drift findings\n505\t          # (ORPHAN-MEDIUM-297). The seeder resolves its output through the\n506\t          # kernel's own `repo_state_root`, so the seeds land in the store the\n507\t          # cycle_guard reads from — writing them to the checkout instead would\n508\t          # be a producer that stays green feeding a consumer that stays empty.\n509\t          python3 tools/aria-poc/seed_drift_findings.py --repo-root \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE\" ${ARIA_SUPERGRAPH:+--supergraph \"$ARIA_SUPERGRAPH\"} | tail -12\n510\t\n511\t      - name: Cycle guard (advisory)\n512\t        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true' && (github.event.inputs.mode || 'cycle') == 'cycle'\n513\t        run: |\n514\t          set -euo pipefail\n515\t          PYTHONPATH=aria-kernel python3 -m aria_kernel cycle-guard evaluate \\\n516\t            --cycle-id \"auto-cycle-${GITHUB_RUN_ID}\" \\\n517\t            --workspace-root \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE\" \\\n518\t            --tools-dir \"$ARIA_TOOLS_DIR\" | tee /tmp/cycle-guard.json || true\n519\t          {\n520\t            echo '### Cycle guard (advisory)'\n521\t            echo '```json'\n522\t            cat /tmp/cycle-guard.json 2>/dev/null || echo '{}'\n523\t            echo '```'\n524\t          } >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"\n525\t\n526\t      # ORPHAN-HIGH-471 — the nightly lane spawns agents through the same\n527\t      # run_claude_exec path the executor lane does, but shipped without a\n528\t      # sandbox backend. bwrap is not present on a bare runner, so\n529\t      # wrap_bash_in_sandbox raises SandboxUnavailable: the lane fails closed\n530\t      # rather than running unconfined, which is correct but means it could\n531\t      # not do real agent work at all. These two steps are copied from\n532\t      # aria-agent-executor.yml so the two lanes cannot drift.\n533\t      #\n534\t      # Gated on effective_mock == 'false', not run unconditionally: a mock\n535\t      # cycle spawns no agent and needs no backend, and a hard verify on a\n536\t      # host without unprivileged user namespaces would turn a working mock\n537\t      # nightly red for a capability it never uses.\n538\t      # RC-9 — one definition, called by both lanes. The pair of steps that\n539\t      # used to sit here was duplicated between aria-agent-executor and\n540\t      # aria-auto-cycle, with a comment saying they were copied so the lanes\n541\t      # could not drift; copying is how they drift. The action also probes\n542\t      # BEFORE installing, so an already-provisioned runner needs no apt call.\n543\t      - name: Ensure a verified sandbox backend\n544\t        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true' && steps.preflight.outputs.effective_mock == 'false'\n545\t        uses: ./.github/actions/ensure-sandbox-backend\n546\t\n547\t      # The adapter manifests declare `npx ts-node …` runners, and the tool\n548\t      # runner refuses to execute them when the workspace has no repo-local\n549\t      # ts-node — correctly: silently resolving a global one would run\n550\t      # unpinned code. This lane never provisioned what the manifests\n551\t      # declare, so the first cycle after the registry sync ran six adapters\n552\t      # into six `tool_unhealthy` rows (missing node_modules/ts-node). The\n553\t      # runner is self-hosted and the checkout persists, so the guard makes\n554\t      # this a no-op on every run after the first.\n555\t      # Operator-gated unquarantine, BEFORE the cycle so the same run's\n556\t      # tools phase can exercise the lifted tools and the same publish step\n557\t      # carries the audited transition. The kernel verb refuses without an\n558\t      # approval ref; empty inputs make this a no-op, so the scheduled\n559\t      # nightly never touches it. Added because six adapters were quarantined\n560\t      # by an environment fault (missing node deps priced as tool guilt) and\n561\t      # unquarantine_tool was API-only — no operator-reachable caller.\n562\t      # ARIA-CRITICAL-216 — each ref is an operator's GitHub act approving one\n563\t      # tool, verified by the kernel through the API and spent once, so the\n564\t      # refs pair with the ids one to one.\n565\t      - name: Operator unquarantine (dispatch-gated)\n566\t        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true' && github.event.inputs.unquarantine_tool_ids != ''\n567\t        env:\n568\t          TOOL_IDS: ${{ github.event.inputs.unquarantine_tool_ids }}\n569\t          APPROVAL_REF: ${{ github.event.inputs.unquarantine_approval_ref }}\n570\t          ARIA_TOOLS: ${{ env.ARIA_TOOLS_DIR }}\n571\t          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}\n572\t        run: |\n573\t          set -euo pipefail\n574\t          [ -n \"${APPROVAL_REF}\" ] || { echo '::error::unquarantine requires an approval ref'; exit 1; }\n575\t          if ! printf '%s' \"${TOOL_IDS}\" | grep -Eq '^[a-z0-9-]+(,[a-z0-9-]+)*$'; then\n576\t            echo '::error::tool ids must be comma-separated slugs'; exit 1\n577\t          fi\n578\t          GH_REF='gh:[A-Za-z0-9-]+/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+#[0-9]+/(comment|review)/[0-9]+'\n579\t          if ! printf '%s' \"${APPROVAL_REF}\" | grep -Eq \"^${GH_REF}(,${GH_REF})*\\$\"; then\n580\t            echo '::error::approval refs must be comma-separated gh:<owner>/<repo>#<n>/comment|review/<id>'; exit 1\n581\t          fi\n582\t          IFS=',' read -ra IDS <<< \"${TOOL_IDS}\"\n583\t          IFS=',' read -ra REFS <<< \"${APPROVAL_REF}\"\n584\t          if [ \"${#IDS[@]}\" -ne \"${#REFS[@]}\" ]; then\n585\t            echo '::error::one approval ref per tool id, in the same order'; exit 1\n586\t          fi\n587\t          for i in \"${!IDS[@]}\"; do\n588\t            PYTHONPATH=aria-kernel python3 -m aria_kernel tool unquarantine \\\n589\t              --tool-id \"${IDS[$i]}\" \\\n590\t              --reason \"operator dispatch: environment-fault quarantine lifted\" \\\n591\t              --operator-approval-ref \"${REFS[$i]}\" \\\n592\t              --root-cause-note \"quarantined by missing workspace node deps (ORPHAN-HIGH-607); the tool never executed\" \\\n593\t              --fixture-update-ref \"n/a: no fixture change; environment fault\" \\\n594\t              --tools-dir \"${ARIA_TOOLS}\"\n595\t          done\n596\t\n597\t      # A2 — one definition, both lanes (RC-9): the inline copy that used to\n598\t      # sit here is now .github/actions/ensure-node-deps, shared with the\n599\t      # executor lane whose missing provisioning tripped FAZ 5's pre-claim\n600\t      # gate (`env_deps_missing`, run 31456376668).\n601\t      - name: Provision the node dependencies the adapter manifests declare\n602\t        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true'\n603\t        uses: ./.github/actions/ensure-node-deps\n604\t\n605\t      # FAZ 5a — the merge gate's attestation producer, at lane start so the\n606\t      # row describes the host that will run the merge. Refusals (no sandbox,"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8106c64f-84fe-4fc2-85a1-3e610198f667","uuid":"03c46242-61dc-4215-9ac0-0349e4152bad","timestamp":"2026-10-06T09:42:25.453Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-primary-planner-1ca79b2cdd2f/.github/workflows/aria-auto-cycle.yml","content":"      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n        with:\n          ref: main\n          persist-credentials: false\n          # The kernel reads this checkout's HISTORY, not only its tree: the\n          # twin's churn and co-change layers replay `git log -n400`, the\n          # incremental refresh asks whether the prior map's anchor commit\n          # exists, the executor's anchor gate asks the same of a previous\n          # run's commit, and intent context reads per-file logs.\n          # actions/checkout defaults to depth 1, and on this persistent\n          # workspace a depth-1 fetch RE-SHALLOWS a full clone, so the run\n          # that wrote the live map (2026-09-12) counted one commit: churn\n          # and co-change never reached their recurrence thresholds and an\n          # empty history layer was published as a healthy map, while every\n          # refresh was a full rebuild (`refresh.reason=unknown_anchor`).\n          # 0 = the whole history; on an already-shallow workspace the\n          # action fetches `--unshallow`. The kernel refuses a shallow\n          # checkout by name (`twin_history_unavailable_shallow`) and\n          # aria-kernel/tests/invariants/test_kernel_lanes_check_out_full_history.py\n          # pins this flag on every lane that runs the kernel. The\n          # aria/state store is NOT this checkout: the restore action\n          # materialises it through the kernel's own fetch (no depth) into a\n          # sibling worktree, and its history length is governed by\n          # aria-state-maintenance, not by this step.\n          fetch-depth: 0\n          # Self-hosted workspace persists between ALL jobs (CI, executor,\n          # this lane). A dispatch-triggered run inherits whatever the\n          # previous job left behind; the mid-run preflight's\n          # workspace_worktree_not_clean guard then rejects the run for\n          # files this lane never created. The reset step above already put\n          # the tree back to the committed state, so checkout does not repeat\n          # it with the one flag that also throws the dependencies away.\n          clean: false\n\n      # ARIA-HIGH-019 — anchor the job's wall-clock at the earliest possible\n      # step, BEFORE checkout and provisioning: every setup minute spent\n      # before the epoch is a minute stolen from the close-out margin. The\n      # nightly step used to compute its seal deadline from its OWN start\n      # time, assuming ~5m of setup before it; a fresh-store restore of the\n      # aria/state branch measures 17m41s (run 32771477211), which spent\n      # the whole close-out margin before the first cycle phase and pushed the\n      # seal 8 minutes past the 360m platform ceiling — the job was cancelled\n      # between seal and publish (again on run 33172463619, cancelled at the\n      # ceiling mid-handoff with the anchor still after setup).\n      - name: Anchor the job launch epoch\n        run: echo \"ARIA_JOB_LAUNCH_EPOCH=$(date +%s)\" >> \"$GITHUB_ENV\"\n\n      - uses: ./.github/actions/setup-aria-kernel\n\n      - uses: actions/setup-node@820762786026740c76f36085b0efc47a31fe5020 # v7.0.0\n        with:\n          node-version: '22'\n\n      # Same dependency step as aria-operational-proof — the fresh\n      # tool-cache Python on the self-hosted runner ships without the\n      # kernel's deps (first live run failed importing yaml inside\n      # verify_workflow_preflight, ORPHAN-LOW-302).\n      - name: Pre-flight - Claude Code CLI present\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          REQUIRED_CLAUDE_VERSION=\"2.1.221\"\n          CLAUDE_VERSION=\"$(claude --version | awk '{print $1}')\"\n          python3 - \"$REQUIRED_CLAUDE_VERSION\" \"$CLAUDE_VERSION\" <<'PY'\n          import sys\n          def parse(value):\n              return tuple(int(part) for part in value.split('.'))\n          required = parse(sys.argv[1])\n          current = parse(sys.argv[2])\n          if current < required:\n              raise SystemExit(f\"claude-code {sys.argv[2]} is below required {sys.argv[1]}\")\n          print(f\"claude-code {sys.argv[2]} satisfies required {sys.argv[1]}\")\n          PY\n\n      - name: Pre-flight - Claude Code auth + mock-mode kill-switch resolution\n        id: preflight\n        env:\n          KILL_SWITCH: ${{ vars.ARIA_MOCK_KILL_SWITCH }}\n          INPUT_MOCK: ${{ github.event.inputs.mock }}\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          if [ -n \"${KILL_SWITCH:-}\" ]; then\n            EFFECTIVE_MOCK=\"${KILL_SWITCH}\"\n            MOCK_SOURCE=\"kill_switch\"\n          elif [ -n \"${INPUT_MOCK:-}\" ]; then\n            EFFECTIVE_MOCK=\"${INPUT_MOCK}\"\n            MOCK_SOURCE=\"workflow_dispatch_input\"\n          else\n            EFFECTIVE_MOCK=\"false\"\n            MOCK_SOURCE=\"workflow_default_claude\"\n          fi\n          # Plan 032 Faz 032a — the kill switch is a raw repository variable\n          # and the kernel's boolean parser raises on anything but\n          # true/false, so a value like `enabled` would not force mock: it\n          # would crash the executor after the claim. Refuse it HERE, where\n          # the operator can read the reason, and name the source.\n          case \"${EFFECTIVE_MOCK}\" in\n            true|false) ;;\n            *)\n              echo \"::error::ARIA mock resolution from ${MOCK_SOURCE} is '${EFFECTIVE_MOCK}'; only 'true' or 'false' is accepted\" >&2\n              exit 1\n              ;;\n          esac\n          echo \"effective_mock=${EFFECTIVE_MOCK}\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n          echo \"mock_source=${MOCK_SOURCE}\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n          if [ \"${EFFECTIVE_MOCK}\" = \"false\" ]; then\n            if env | grep -E '^(ANTHROPIC_API_KEY|CLAUDE_API_KEY|ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN)=' >/dev/null; then\n              echo \"::error::API-key Claude mode is disallowed for ARIA; use a managed Claude Code login on the trusted runner.\" >&2\n              exit 1\n            fi\n            claude --version >/tmp/aria-claude-auth.txt 2>/tmp/aria-claude-auth.err \\\n              || { echo \"::error::Claude Code CLI preflight failed\" >&2; cat /tmp/aria-claude-auth.err >&2; exit 1; }\n            CLAUDE_AUTH_DIR=\"${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}\"\n            if [ ! -f \"${CLAUDE_AUTH_DIR}/.credentials.json\" ] && [ ! -f \"${CLAUDE_AUTH_DIR}/config.json\" ]; then\n              echo \"::error::Claude Code managed-auth session not found on the trusted runner\" >&2\n              exit 1\n            fi\n          fi\n\n      - name: Persist enterprise workflow preflight\n        env:\n          PYTHONPATH: aria-kernel\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          python3 - <<'PY'\n          import os\n          from pathlib import Path\n\n          from aria_kernel.preflight import verify_workflow_preflight\n          from aria_kernel.runtime_profile import SCHEDULER_MAX_PROPOSABLE_PROFILE\n\n          # PLAN Wave 1 PR 2.6b — ONE declared write root, because after the\n          # cutover there is one. The cycle's three state roots (tools,\n          # workspace, findings) all live inside the store worktree, so the\n          # pair of checkout-relative roots this used to declare would now\n          # be write roots nothing writes to — a declaration that stopped\n          # describing the run. Everything outside the store stays read-only,\n          # which is a STRONGER claim than before: `aria-findings/` in the\n          # checkout is no longer written at all.\n          allowed = [\".aria-state-store\"]\n          verdict = verify_workflow_preflight(\n              workflow_id=\"aria-auto-cycle\",\n              job_id=\"cycle\",\n              # ORPHAN-HIGH-728 — this preflight runs BEFORE the state store\n              # is bound, so it cannot know tonight's profile; what it can\n              # declare honestly is the lane's STRUCTURAL ceiling, the most\n              # any resolution here could ever produce. Read from the kernel\n              # rather than written as a literal, so a constant that moves\n              # moves this declaration with it. The profile the run actually\n              # took is declared by the second preflight after the resolver,\n              # and recorded by `set_profile` in runtime-profile-history.jsonl.\n              profile=SCHEDULER_MAX_PROPOSABLE_PROFILE,\n              workspace_root=os.environ[\"GITHUB_WORKSPACE\"],\n              allowed_write_roots=allowed,\n              path_allowlist=allowed,\n              # `github_git` is what the cutover added to this lane's reach:\n              # the state branch is fetched and pushed over the repository\n              # remote. Declared rather than folded into `github_artifact`,\n              # because they are different credentials with different blast\n              # radii and a reviewer must be able to see the git one.\n              network_policy=[\"github_artifact\", \"github_git\"],\n              network_enforcement_evidence=(\n                  \"state restore/publish limited to the aria/state branch over \"\n                  \"the repository remote; artifact upload limited to the \"\n                  \"run-scoped aria-state-cache-<run_id> and \"\n                  \"quarantine-evidence-<run_id> names, both via the Actions \"\n                  \"artifact API\"\n              ),\n              token_provenance=\"github_actions_artifact_token\",\n              require_github_app=False,\n              dlp_mode=\"fail_closed\",\n              dlp_scan_clean=True,\n              audit_reason=(\n                  \"nightly producer cycle over the durable aria/state store; \"\n                  \"profile resolved per run from the L1 autonomy ladder \"\n                  \"(strict when valid, standard otherwise)\"\n              ),\n              audit_artifact_path=Path(os.environ[\"RUNNER_TEMP\"]) / \"aria-auto-cycle-preflight.json\",\n              external_root_allowlist=[str(Path(os.environ[\"RUNNER_TEMP\"]).resolve())],\n          )\n          if not verdict.valid:\n              raise SystemExit(\";\".join(verdict.reasons))\n          PY\n\n      - name: Restore ARIA state from the aria/state branch\n        id: restore_state\n        # RC-6 — one definition, both lanes. The two hand-copied heredocs that\n        # used to sit here had already drifted: only the executor's carried the\n        # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-484/488 restore-proof outputs, so the producer lane\n        # could publish a bootstrap-empty tree over the accumulated state. The\n        # action's header records what that cost.\n        #\n        # PLAN Wave 1 PR 2.6b — the transport is the durable store now, not the\n        # 30-day artifact. The output contract is unchanged, deliberately: both\n        # publish gates below still read `restored` / `bootstrap`. The action\n        # also EXPORTS the store binding into the job environment, which is why\n        # every `--tools-dir` below names \"$ARIA_TOOLS_DIR\" and never a literal.\n        uses: ./.github/actions/restore-aria-state\n        with:\n          bootstrap-ack: ${{ vars.ARIA_STATE_BOOTSTRAP_ACK }}\n          # ARIA-HIGH-342 — this job writes aria/state, so it takes the writer\n          # lease before the checkout and holds it to the release step at the\n          # end: no other lane can publish in between. The expiry is this\n          # job's own timeout-minutes plus the 80-minute leased-publish margin\n          # (GSEC-MEDIUM-003); the wait covers the hosted writers' whole\n          # bounds (at most 25 min) before the run yields by name.\n          writer-lease-ttl-minutes: '440'\n          writer-lease-wait-seconds: '1800'\n      - name: Pre-flight - cross-host autonomous-loop lease check\n        id: lease_check\n        env:\n          WRITER_LEASE: ${{ steps.restore_state.outputs.writer-lease }}\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          PYTHONPATH=aria-kernel python3 - <<'PY' 2>&1 | head -20\n          import json\n          import os\n          import sys\n\n          from aria_kernel.autonomous_host_lease import lease_state\n\n          # ARIA-HIGH-342 — no writer lease, no store: the restore yielded\n          # (another lane is writing aria/state) and checked nothing out, so\n          # this run blocks here, before anything reads the tree.\n          if os.environ.get('WRITER_LEASE') != 'held':\n              print('::warning::aria/state writer lease not held; run yielded', file=sys.stderr)\n              with open(os.environ['GITHUB_OUTPUT'], 'a', encoding='utf-8') as f:\n                  f.write('blocked=true\\n')\n                  f.write('blocked_host=aria/state-writer-lease\\n')\n              sys.exit(0)\n\n          # os.environ[...] rather than a literal: the lease ledger lives in\n          # the store, and a KeyError here is the correct outcome if the\n          # restore did not bind it. The literal would have read a DIFFERENT,\n          # empty lease ledger in the ephemeral checkout and reported \"no\n          # lease held\" — the exact false clear ORPHAN-CRITICAL-469 was.\n          state = lease_state(os.environ['ARIA_TOOLS_DIR'])\n          print(json.dumps(state))\n          if state['state'] == 'fresh':\n              host = state.get('host_id', '')\n              if not host.startswith('gha-runner:'):\n                  print(f'::warning::autonomous_host_lease_blocked: held by {host}', file=sys.stderr)\n                  with open(os.environ['GITHUB_OUTPUT'], 'a', encoding='utf-8') as f:\n                      f.write('blocked=true\\n')\n                      f.write(f'blocked_host={host}\\n')\n                  sys.exit(0)\n          with open(os.environ['GITHUB_OUTPUT'], 'a', encoding='utf-8') as f:\n              f.write('blocked=false\\n')\n          PY\n\n      - name: Skip when local lease is fresh\n        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked == 'true'\n        run: |\n          echo \"Local host holds autonomous-loop lease; exiting cleanly per Plan ARIA-V3 §2n.\"\n          exit 0\n\n      # Every `--tools-dir` below names \"$ARIA_TOOLS_DIR\", the binding the\n      # restore step exported, and never the literal `aria-tools`. The literal\n      # is not merely stale — `aria-tools/repo_identity.json` is TRACKED in the\n      # checkout, so it names a real, valid, and completely wrong tools root:\n      # the run would succeed while writing every ledger to a tree that dies\n      # with the runner. `set -u` turns an absent binding into a failed step,\n      # which is the only acceptable alternative to the right root.\n      #\n      # The `integrity migrate-tools-bootstrap` step that used to sit here moved\n      # INTO the restore action (PLAN Wave 1 PR 2.6b). It was this lane's alone,\n      # and the executor needed it just as much: a store checkout is not a\n      # usable tools root until it is bound. One definition, both lanes — the\n      # same correction RC-6 made to the restore itself.\n      - name: Handoff snapshot - session_start\n        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true'\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          PYTHONPATH=aria-kernel python3 -m aria_kernel handoff snapshot \\\n            --session-id \"auto-cycle-${GITHUB_RUN_ID}\" \\\n            --tools-dir \"$ARIA_TOOLS_DIR\" \\\n            --trigger session_start\n\n      - name: Fetch the composed supergraph for the drift scan\n        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true' && (github.event.inputs.mode || 'cycle') == 'cycle'\n        env:\n          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          # ARIA-HIGH-333 — the scan judges a UI option list against what the GraphQL transport carries, which it\n          # reads from the composed supergraph. This host does not compose one; the artifact CI composed for HEAD's\n          # exact schema is fetched instead. Without it the scan reports wire_unverifiable rather than guessing.\n          SG_DIR=\"${RUNNER_TEMP}/aria-supergraph\"\n          if out=\"$(python3 tools/aria-poc/fetch_supergraph.py --repo-root \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE\" --repo \"$GITHUB_REPOSITORY\" --out-dir \"$SG_DIR\")\"; then\n            echo \"ARIA_SUPERGRAPH=${SG_DIR}/supergraph.graphql\" >> \"$GITHUB_ENV\"\n          fi\n          echo \"$out\"\n\n      - name: Seed drift findings from fresh scan\n        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true' && (github.event.inputs.mode || 'cycle') == 'cycle'\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          # A fresh PoC mechanical scan at HEAD feeds the empty-cycle guard and\n          # the pressure pipeline with real, never-stale drift findings\n          # (ORPHAN-MEDIUM-297). The seeder resolves its output through the\n          # kernel's own `repo_state_root`, so the seeds land in the store the\n          # cycle_guard reads from — writing them to the checkout instead would\n          # be a producer that stays green feeding a consumer that stays empty.\n          python3 tools/aria-poc/seed_drift_findings.py --repo-root \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE\" ${ARIA_SUPERGRAPH:+--supergraph \"$ARIA_SUPERGRAPH\"} | tail -12\n\n      - name: Cycle guard (advisory)\n        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true' && (github.event.inputs.mode || 'cycle') == 'cycle'\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          PYTHONPATH=aria-kernel python3 -m aria_kernel cycle-guard evaluate \\\n            --cycle-id \"auto-cycle-${GITHUB_RUN_ID}\" \\\n            --workspace-root \"$GITHUB_WORKSPACE\" \\\n            --tools-dir \"$ARIA_TOOLS_DIR\" | tee /tmp/cycle-guard.json || true\n          {\n            echo '### Cycle guard (advisory)'\n            echo '```json'\n            cat /tmp/cycle-guard.json 2>/dev/null || echo '{}'\n            echo '```'\n          } >> \"$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\"\n\n      # ORPHAN-HIGH-471 — the nightly lane spawns agents through the same\n      # run_claude_exec path the executor lane does, but shipped without a\n      # sandbox backend. bwrap is not present on a bare runner, so\n      # wrap_bash_in_sandbox raises SandboxUnavailable: the lane fails closed\n      # rather than running unconfined, which is correct but means it could\n      # not do real agent work at all. These two steps are copied from\n      # aria-agent-executor.yml so the two lanes cannot drift.\n      #\n      # Gated on effective_mock == 'false', not run unconditionally: a mock\n      # cycle spawns no agent and needs no backend, and a hard verify on a\n      # host without unprivileged user namespaces would turn a working mock\n      # nightly red for a capability it never uses.\n      # RC-9 — one definition, called by both lanes. The pair of steps that\n      # used to sit here was duplicated between aria-agent-executor and\n      # aria-auto-cycle, with a comment saying they were copied so the lanes\n      # could not drift; copying is how they drift. The action also probes\n      # BEFORE installing, so an already-provisioned runner needs no apt call.\n      - name: Ensure a verified sandbox backend\n        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true' && steps.preflight.outputs.effective_mock == 'false'\n        uses: ./.github/actions/ensure-sandbox-backend\n\n      # The adapter manifests declare `npx ts-node …` runners, and the tool\n      # runner refuses to execute them when the workspace has no repo-local\n      # ts-node — correctly: silently resolving a global one would run\n      # unpinned code. This lane never provisioned what the manifests\n      # declare, so the first cycle after the registry sync ran six adapters\n      # into six `tool_unhealthy` rows (missing node_modules/ts-node). The\n      # runner is self-hosted and the checkout persists, so the guard makes\n      # this a no-op on every run after the first.\n      # Operator-gated unquarantine, BEFORE the cycle so the same run's\n      # tools phase can exercise the lifted tools and the same publish step\n      # carries the audited transition. The kernel verb refuses without an\n      # approval ref; empty inputs make this a no-op, so the scheduled\n      # nightly never touches it. Added because six adapters were quarantined\n      # by an environment fault (missing node deps priced as tool guilt) and\n      # unquarantine_tool was API-only — no operator-reachable caller.\n      # ARIA-CRITICAL-216 — each ref is an operator's GitHub act approving one\n      # tool, verified by the kernel through the API and spent once, so the\n      # refs pair with the ids one to one.\n      - name: Operator unquarantine (dispatch-gated)\n        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true' && github.event.inputs.unquarantine_tool_ids != ''\n        env:\n          TOOL_IDS: ${{ github.event.inputs.unquarantine_tool_ids }}\n          APPROVAL_REF: ${{ github.event.inputs.unquarantine_approval_ref }}\n          ARIA_TOOLS: ${{ env.ARIA_TOOLS_DIR }}\n          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}\n        run: |\n          set -euo pipefail\n          [ -n \"${APPROVAL_REF}\" ] || { echo '::error::unquarantine requires an approval ref'; exit 1; }\n          if ! printf '%s' \"${TOOL_IDS}\" | grep -Eq '^[a-z0-9-]+(,[a-z0-9-]+)*$'; then\n            echo '::error::tool ids must be comma-separated slugs'; exit 1\n          fi\n          GH_REF='gh:[A-Za-z0-9-]+/[A-Za-z0-9._-]+#[0-9]+/(comment|review)/[0-9]+'\n          if ! printf '%s' \"${APPROVAL_REF}\" | grep -Eq \"^${GH_REF}(,${GH_REF})*\\$\"; then\n            echo '::error::approval refs must be comma-separated gh:<owner>/<repo>#<n>/comment|review/<id>'; exit 1\n          fi\n          IFS=',' read -ra IDS <<< \"${TOOL_IDS}\"\n          IFS=',' read -ra REFS <<< \"${APPROVAL_REF}\"\n          if [ \"${#IDS[@]}\" -ne \"${#REFS[@]}\" ]; then\n            echo '::error::one approval ref per tool id, in the same order'; exit 1\n          fi\n          for i in \"${!IDS[@]}\"; do\n            PYTHONPATH=aria-kernel python3 -m aria_kernel tool unquarantine \\\n              --tool-id \"${IDS[$i]}\" \\\n              --reason \"operator dispatch: environment-fault quarantine lifted\" \\\n              --operator-approval-ref \"${REFS[$i]}\" \\\n              --root-cause-note \"quarantined by missing workspace node deps (ORPHAN-HIGH-607); the tool never executed\" \\\n              --fixture-update-ref \"n/a: no fixture change; environment fault\" \\\n              --tools-dir \"${ARIA_TOOLS}\"\n          done\n\n      # A2 — one definition, both lanes (RC-9): the inline copy that used to\n      # sit here is now .github/actions/ensure-node-deps, shared with the\n      # executor lane whose missing provisioning tripped FAZ 5's pre-claim\n      # gate (`env_deps_missing`, run 31456376668).\n      - name: Provision the node dependencies the adapter manifests declare\n        if: steps.lease_check.outputs.blocked != 'true'\n        uses: ./.github/actions/ensure-node-deps\n\n      # FAZ 5a — the merge gate's attestation producer, at lane start so the\n      # row describes the host that will run the merge. Refusals (no sandbox,","numLines":400,"startLine":207,"totalLines":1149}}}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CfkmAPXVrkmiVjwnZdAWM","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01HqRAEHob7cGH5jtJgeyNX5","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-primary-planner-1ca79b2cdd2f/.github/workflows/aria-auto-cycle.yml","offset":607,"limit":260},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":111853,"cache_read_input_tokens":0,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":111853},"output_tokens":5,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8106c64f-84fe-4fc2-85a1-3e610198f667","uuid":"53483510-59cf-408c-8a82-d37ed2027304","timestamp":"2026-10-06T09:42:25.991Z","request_id":"req_011CfkmAP5huHCGG3A3ppUZS","wire_tool_inputs":{"toolu_01HqRAEHob7cGH5jtJgeyNX5":{"file_path":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-primary-planner-1ca79b2cdd2f/.github/workflows/aria-auto-cycle.yml","offset":607,"limit":260}}}
